        "title": "Public Transport in Rio de Janeiro: An Expat's 2026 Guide - The Rio Times",
        "description": "# Getting Around Rio: Metro, Buses and Apps, Made Simple\n## Key Takeaways\n- Analyze the Municipal Transport Secretariat’s oversight of the 2026 integrated transit network to streamline logistics for international residents.\n- Pinpoint high-efficiency routes on MetrôRio Lines One, Two, and Four that connect premium residential areas to the city’s core business districts.\n- Examine the role of the VLT and BRT corridors in the revitalization of Porto Maravilha and the expansion of connectivity in the West Zone.\n- Navigate the 2026 fare landscape and the mandatory RioCard Mais system to manage transit costs effectively in R$ with USD equivalents.\n- Evaluate the latest security data and the impact of the “Segurança Presente” initiative on the reliability of public transport Rio de Janeiro.\n\n## The 2026 Landscape of Public Transport Rio de Janeiro\n### The Insider-Outsider Perspective on Commuting\nCommuting experiences vary based on local geography and socioeconomic status. Residents in the South Zone benefit from the highest density of stations and frequent bus service. Instead, those in the North Zone often face longer wait times on the SuperVia rail lines or crowded BRT buses. Expats and international investors typically favor the Metro due to its safety and superior air conditioning. However, the SMTR reported that 62% of car owners now use public transport Rio de Janeiro for their daily commute to the Centro district. High fuel prices and new congestion pricing zones have driven this shift toward rail and light rail options.\n\n## The MetrôRio Network: Efficient Public Transport Rio de Janeiro for Business Districts\n### Operational Hours and Peak Performance\nSunday service runs from 7:00 AM to 11:00 PM. During peak morning and evening hours, train frequency on shared tracks reaches intervals of less than five minutes. Security personnel enforce these regulations strictly at every station. Passengers can check the [official MetrôRio fares and payment systems](https://www.metrorio.com.br/) to confirm current costs, which stood at R$7.50 (~$1.35) in early 2024. This pricing structure remains competitive compared to private ride-sharing apps during surge pricing events.\n\nWatch the development of the Gávea station project throughout 2025. This unfinished hub remains a critical piece of the mobility puzzle for the South Zone. The state government is currently negotiating new funding models to resume construction. Similarly, keep an eye on the potential renewal of the MetrôRio concession agreement. These negotiations will likely dictate the next decade of **public transport Rio de Janeiro** infrastructure upgrades. Investors should monitor these legislative shifts, as they directly impact the city’s long-term urban development and economic trajectory.\n\n![Navigating Public Transport Rio de Janeiro: A 2026 Guide for Expats and Investors](https://www.riotimesonline.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Navigating-Public-Transport-Rio-de-Janeiro-A-2026-Guide-for-Expats-and-Investors-Infographic.jpg)\n\n## VLT and BRT: Connecting Porto Maravilha and the West Zone\n### The BRT System: Challenges and 2026 Solutions\nThe BRT (Bus Rapid Transit) network covers more than 120 kilometers across the city. It includes three main corridors: TransOeste, TransCarioca, and TransOlímpica. These dedicated lanes allow high-capacity articulated buses to bypass heavy traffic in the West Zone. However, the system faced severe maintenance issues and fleet shortages between 2018 and 2021. Investors seeking Brazilian infrastructure updates should follow [The Rio Times](https://www.riotimesonline.com/).\n\nMayor Eduardo Paes initiated a complete overhaul of the BRT in 2022. The city purchased over 700 new yellow buses to replace the aging fleet. By 2026, the City Hall plans to introduce 100 electric buses into the network. This R$2 billion (~$360 million) investment aims to lower carbon emissions and improve passenger comfort. Simultaneously, the State Government increased the presence of the “BRT Seguro” police force. This unit improves [Safety and reliability on Rio’s public transport](https://travel.gc.ca/destinations/brazil) by preventing vandalism and fare evasion at the 130 stations.\n\nThe TransCarioca line provides a vital link for international travelers. It connects the Galeão International Airport (GIG) to the metro system at the Vicente de Carvalho station. This route offers a cost-effective alternative to private transfers. Expats can find more logistics tips in our [Rio Living Guide](https://www.riotimesonline.com/). The BRT remains the most practical way to reach the Barra da Tijuca region from the North Zone.\n\nThe next two years will be critical for the stabilization of these transit corridors. Observers should watch for the completion of the TransBrasil corridor in late 2025. This fourth line will connect Deodoro to the downtown area via the Avenida Brasil highway. The integration of this final corridor will likely redefine the flow of **public transport Rio de Janeiro** for the next decade. Success depends on the continued cooperation between the Mobi-Rio agency and state security forces.\n\n## The Economics of Public Transport Rio de Janeiro: Fares and Payment Systems\nRio de Janeiro’s transit network operates under a complex pricing model influenced by municipal subsidies and inflationary pressures. The Central Bank of Brazil highlighted in its 2025 quarterly report that service price volatility remains a primary driver for urban cost-of-living indices. Consequently, for those navigating public transport Rio de Janeiro, understanding the payment architecture is essential for fiscal planning. The city’s administration frequently adjusts rates to balance operational costs against the purchasing power of its residents.\n\nBy early 2026, the projected fare for a single Metro or municipal bus trip is R$8.20 (~$1.45). This price point reflects a steady climb from previous years as the city adjusts to rising energy costs. While cash is still accepted on most municipal buses, the system is rapidly moving toward a completely cashless environment. Therefore, travelers must prepare before reaching the turnstiles to avoid delays.\n\nMost turnstiles in the Metro and VLT systems now feature integrated NFC technology. This convenience eliminates the need for physical tickets for short-term visitors. However, residents and long-term expats find the RioCard Mais provides superior integration between different modes of transport. This card allows for discounted transfers between buses, trains, and ferries within specific time windows.\n\n### Acquiring and Topping Up Your RioCard\nThe RioCard Mais is the primary tool for seamless transit across the city’s buses, trains, and ferries. Users can purchase these physical cards at major Metro stations, such as Carioca or Jardim Oceânico, and at dedicated RioCard kiosks. The initial card cost is typically R$4.00 (~$0.70). This small fee acts as a deposit for the physical hardware. For daily Ibovespa analysis, see [The Rio Times Market Reports](https://www.riotimesonline.com/premium-membership/).\n\nDigital management has become the standard for tech-savvy commuters in Brazil. After payment, the credit must be validated at a terminal. These validation points are found on every bus and at station entrances. Instead of waiting in long queues at ticket windows, expats can manage their entire transit budget from a smartphone. This digital shift has reduced station congestion by 22% since its implementation began.\n\nLooking ahead, the city administration plans to expand the “Bilhete Único” integration to include private shuttle services by late 2026. This move aims to further reduce the financial burden on commuters traveling from the West Zone. Investors should monitor municipal legislative shifts regarding transit subsidies. These decisions will dictate the long-term stability of fare prices in the coming decade. Maintaining an efficient transport budget is a cornerstone of financial success for any expat living in the Marvelous City.\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions About Public Transport in Rio de Janeiro\n### Can I pay for the bus with a credit card in Rio?\nMost municipal buses in Rio de Janeiro now accept contactless credit and debit cards for fare payment. This technological upgrade, managed by the municipal transport secretariat, covers over 90% of the city’s fleet. However, some older feeder lines in the North Zone still require a physical RioCard or cash. Consequently, carrying a small amount of Brazilian Real remains a prudent strategy for suburban travel.\n\n## You've reached your free article limit\nComing up in Rio de Janeiro\n\nLiving in Rio de Janeiro\n\nUnderstand Brazil",

Nomads](https://www.riotimesonline.com/expats-and-nomads/) Expats in Brazil\n\n# Getting Around Rio: Metro, Buses and Apps, Made Simple\n\nBy [Matthias Camenzind](https://www.riotimesonline.com/author/matthias-camenzind/)·March 10, 2026·19 min read\n\nSaveShareIn shortContentsListenAa\n\n![Navigating Public Transport Rio de Janeiro: A 2026 Guide for Expats and Investors](https://www.riotimesonline.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Navigating-Public-Transport-Rio-de-Janeiro-A-2026-Guide-for-Expats-and-Investors-Hero-Image.jpg)\n\nNearly 150,000 daily commuters will utilize the optimized Transbrasil corridor by June 15, 2026. This expansion highlights the evolving landscape of public transport Rio de Janeiro as city authorities prioritize digital integration. Many expats find the local transit network intimidating due to fragmented English data. However, efficient mobility remains essential for those managing assets across the Marvelous City. Consequently, navigating the urban core requires precise data on safety and costs.\n\nIt’s understandable to feel overwhelmed by the intricate RioCard Mais system and shifting fare structures. Therefore, this guide offers a data-driven breakdown of the 2026 transit network to ensure secure travel. Readers will gain a clear understanding of the R$4,30 (~$0.78) base fare and digital payment methods. For daily Ibovespa analysis, see [The Rio Times Market Reports](https://www.riotimesonline.com/). In addition, the analysis covers MetroRio expansion and the latest security protocols implemented by the municipal government.\n\n## Key Takeaways\n\n- Analyze the Municipal Transport Secretariat’s oversight of the 2026 integrated transit network to streamline logistics for international residents.\n- Pinpoint high-efficiency routes on MetrôRio Lines One, Two, and Four that connect premium residential areas to the city’s core business districts.\n- Examine the role of the VLT and BRT corridors in the revitalization of Porto Maravilha and the expansion of connectivity in the West Zone.\n- Navigate the 2026 fare landscape and the mandatory RioCard Mais system to manage transit costs effectively in R$ with USD equivalents.\n- Evaluate the latest security data and the impact of the “Segurança Presente” initiative on the reliability of public transport Rio de Janeiro.\n\n## The 2026 Landscape of Public Transport Rio de Janeiro\n\nRio de Janeiro’s transit network in 2026 operates as a cohesive, multi-modal grid. The Municipal Transport Secretariat (SMTR) provides strict oversight for this entire ecosystem. This agency coordinates the MetrôRio, the VLT light rail, and the extensive BRT corridors to ensure efficiency. Because of these efforts, public transport Rio de Janeiro has become more reliable for the city’s 6.7 million residents. Digital-first boarding is now the primary entry method across all platforms. Most commuters use contactless payments or mobile apps for 94% of their daily trips. This tech-heavy approach has eliminated the need for physical cash on 85% of municipal bus lines. Therefore, boarding efficiency has improved by 18% since early 2024.\n\n### The Evolution of Urban Mobility in Rio\n\nThe 2016 Olympics left a lasting legacy on the current infrastructure. Projects like the TransCarioca and the [Rio de Janeiro Metro network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_de_Janeiro_Metro) expansion still serve as the system’s backbone. By January 15, 2026, total daily ridership hit 3.9 million passengers. This marks a 12% rise from the figures recorded three years prior. The Bilhete Único Carioca remains the central integration tool for the local population. It allows a single fare of R$4.30 (~$0.77) for two separate trips within a three-hour window. This system helps low-income workers manage their daily costs while navigating the vast metropolitan area. Consequently, the SMTR reported that 72% of all commutes now involve at least one transfer between different modes.\n\nBrazil Election Data Room\n\nRunoff poll average: Flávio Bolsonaro 51.0%, Lula 49.0%\n\n45 national polls from 21 pollsters since 4 October, updated hourly. Every poll, free to download.\n\n[Open the Data Room](https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil-election-data-room/) · [Download the CSV](https://www.riotimesonline.com/wp-content/uploads/rt-live/br-runoff-polls.csv) · [Embed the chart](https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil-election-data-room/)\n\n### The Insider-Outsider Perspective on Commuting\n\nCommuting experiences vary based on local geography and socioeconomic status. Residents in the South Zone benefit from the highest density of stations and frequent bus service. Instead, those in the North Zone often face longer wait times on the SuperVia rail lines or crowded BRT buses. Expats and international investors typically favor the Metro due to its safety and superior air conditioning. Ride-sharing apps still compete for passengers during off-peak hours. However, the SMTR reported that 62% of car owners now use public transport Rio de Janeiro for their daily commute to the Centro district. High fuel prices and new congestion pricing zones have driven this shift toward rail and light rail options.\n\n### The LatAm Brief\n\n**One email, every weekday morning.** What moved in Latin American markets, politics and expat life.\n\nYesterday’s subject line: “Lula re-elected in Brazil with 51.8% of valid votes”\n\nGet the brief\n\nFree. We send a confirmation link first — nothing arrives until you click it. Unsubscribe with one click in any edition.\n**If you stop opening us for 30 days we stop sending by ourselves, as we assume the interest is no longer there.**\nSee our [privacy policy](https://www.riotimesonline.com/privacy-policy/). We never share your email.\n\nThe Getulio Vargas Foundation (FGV) recently analyzed these urban trends. Their researchers found that 71% of residents prioritize speed over comfort when choosing a transit mode. “The fiscal health of the city depends on a functioning transport grid that connects the periphery to the economic center,” an FGV analyst stated in a January 2026 report. For daily Ibovespa analysis, see The Rio Times Market Reports. While the system has improved, the gap between the luxury of the South Zone and the utilitarian North Zone remains a point of political debate. Despite these challenges, the integration of digital payments has made the system more accessible to short-term visitors and foreign diplomats alike.\n\nMarket analysts expect the city to announce a new tender for the VLT expansion into the South Zone by late 2026. This project aims to connect the Santos Dumont Airport directly to Ipanema. Investors should watch for upcoming legislative votes regarding private-sector subsidies for the BRT fleet’s electrification. These developments will likely determine the trajectory of Rio’s urban mobility for the next decade.\n\n## The MetrôRio Network: Efficient Public Transport Rio de Janeiro for Business Districts\n\nMetrôRio serves as the primary artery for the city’s professional class. Specifically, Lines 1 and 2 facilitate the movement of roughly 600,000 passengers every day. The system links the affluent residential pockets of Ipanema and Leblon directly to the Centro financial district. Commuters reach the Carioca or Cinelândia stations in under 20 minutes from the South Zone. This efficiency contrasts sharply with the unpredictable traffic on the Aterro do Flamengo. Consequently, reliability remains high. MetrôRio maintains a 99% punctuality rate according to recent reports from AGETRANSP, the state transport regulatory agency. This performance makes it the most dependable form of **public transport Rio de Janeiro** offers for time-sensitive schedules.\n\nThe network utilizes a fleet of 49 trains, including modern Chinese built CNR units. These trains feature advanced braking systems and automated speed control to ensure passenger safety. Security remains a top priority for the concessionaire. Therefore, over 2,000 surveillance cameras monitor stations and cars around the clock. “The efficiency of Rio’s metro system is a cornerstone for regional economic productivity,” states a 2023 infrastructure analysis from the Rio de Janeiro Federation of Industries (FIRJAN). This stability attracts international professionals who require consistent transit options between their residences and corporate offices.\n\n### Line 4: Connecting the South Zone to Barra da Tijuca\n\nLine 4 represents a R$9.7 billion (~$1.8 billion) investment completed for the 2016 Olympic Games. It serves as a vital link for professionals living in the Western expansion zone. Specifically, the Jardim Oceânico station acts as the terminal hub for this line. Here, passengers transfer seamlessly to the BRT corridor. This integration reduces travel time from Barra to Centro by approximately 30 minutes. Consequently, the expansion has bolstered property values along the coastal corridor. For detailed infrastructure investment updates, see [The Rio Times Business Section](https://www.riotimesonline.com/). Many executives now prefer this route over the congested Lagoa-Barra motorway. Therefore, Line 4 has transformed the daily logistics of the city’s high-income workforce.\n\n### Operational Hours and Peak Performance\n\nThe network operates from 5:00 AM to midnight on weekdays and Saturdays. Sunday service runs from 7:00 AM to 11:00 PM. During peak morning and evening hours, train frequency on shared tracks reaches intervals of less than five minutes. All cars feature high-capacity air conditioning and Wi-Fi connectivity. To enhance safety, “Women Only” cars are mandatory on weekdays from 6:00 AM to 9:00 AM and 5:00 PM to 8:00 PM. Security personnel enforce these regulations strictly at every station. Passengers can check the [official MetrôRio fares and payment systems](https://www.metrorio.com.br/) to confirm current costs, which stood at R$7.50 (~$1.35) in early 2024. This pricing structure remains competitive compared to private ride-sharing apps during surge pricing events.\n\nWatch the development of the Gávea station project throughout 2025. This unfinished hub remains a critical piece of the mobility puzzle for the South Zone. The state government is currently negotiating new funding models to resume construction. Similarly, keep an eye on the potential renewal of the MetrôRio concession agreement. These negotiations will likely dictate the next decade of **public transport Rio de Janeiro** infrastructure upgrades. Investors should monitor these legislative shifts, as they directly impact the city’s long-term urban development and economic trajectory.\n\n![Navigating Public Transport Rio de Janeiro: A 2026 Guide for Expats and Investors](https://www.riotimesonline.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Navigating-Public-Transport-Rio-de-Janeiro-A-2026-Guide-for-Expats-and-Investors-Infographic.jpg)\n\n## VLT and BRT: Connecting Porto Maravilha and the West Zone\n\nThe VLT (Veículo Leve sobre Trilhos) functions as the structural centerpiece of the Porto Maravilha revitalization project. This light rail system modernized the historic center by replacing old bus routes with silent, electric tracks. Consequently, it has transformed the downtown commute into a more efficient experience. This network represents a significant shift toward sustainable **public transport Rio de Janeiro** infrastructure. City Hall invested R$1.2 billion (~$218 million) to ensure the system met international standards for urban mobility.\n\n### The VLT: Rio’s Modern Downtown Connector\n\nThe VLT operates three distinct lines. These routes connect the Santos Dumont Airport to the Novo Rio Bus Terminal and the Central do Brasil train station. Because the cars run on a ground-level power supply, there are no overhead wires to clutter the historic skyline. Passengers pay a flat fare of R$4.30 (~$0.78) using a RioCard or the newer Jaé card. For daily Ibovespa analysis, see [The Rio Times Market Reports](https://www.riotimesonline.com/).\n\nThe system relies on a “trust-based” payment model. You must validate your card at the stations or inside the cars before the doors close. Ticket inspectors perform random checks throughout the day. Therefore, you should always keep your card ready for verification. Failure to validate results in a R$170 (~$31) fine. This model reduces boarding times and mirrors successful transit systems in Europe. It serves as a primary example of how the city integrates technology with urban design.\n\n### The BRT System: Challenges and 2026 Solutions\n\nThe BRT (Bus Rapid Transit) network covers more than 120 kilometers across the city. It includes three main corridors: TransOeste, TransCarioca, and TransOlímpica. These dedicated lanes allow high-capacity articulated buses to bypass heavy traffic in the West Zone. However, the system faced severe maintenance issues and fleet shortages between 2018 and 2021. Investors seeking Brazilian infrastructure updates should follow [The Rio Times](https://www.riotimesonline.com/).\n\nMayor Eduardo Paes initiated a complete overhaul of the BRT in 2022. The city purchased over 700 new yellow buses to replace the aging fleet. By 2026, the City Hall plans to introduce 100 electric buses into the network. This R$2 billion (~$360 million) investment aims to lower carbon emissions and improve passenger comfort. Simultaneously, the State Government increased the presence of the “BRT Seguro” police force. This unit improves [Safety and reliability on Rio’s public transport](https://travel.gc.ca/destinations/brazil) by preventing vandalism and fare evasion at the 130 stations.\n\nThe TransCarioca line provides a vital link for international travelers. It connects the Galeão International Airport (GIG) to the metro system at the Vicente de Carvalho station. This route offers a cost-effective alternative to private transfers. Expats can find more logistics tips in our [Rio Living Guide](https://www.riotimesonline.com/). The BRT remains the most practical way to reach the Barra da Tijuca region from the North Zone.\n\nThe next two years will be critical for the stabilization of these transit corridors. Observers should watch for the completion of the TransBrasil corridor in late 2025. This fourth line will connect Deodoro to the downtown area via the Avenida Brasil highway. The integration of this final corridor will likely redefine the flow of **public transport Rio de Janeiro** for the next decade. Success depends on the continued cooperation between the Mobi-Rio agency and state security forces.\n\n## The Economics of Public Transport Rio de Janeiro: Fares and Payment Systems\n\nRio de Janeiro’s transit network operates under a complex pricing model influenced by municipal subsidies and inflationary pressures. The Central Bank of Brazil highlighted in its 2025 quarterly report that service price volatility remains a primary driver for urban cost-of-living indices. Consequently, for those navigating public transport Rio de Janeiro, understanding the payment architecture is essential for fiscal planning. The city’s administration frequently adjusts rates to balance operational costs against the purchasing power of its residents.\n\nBy early 2026, the projected fare for a single Metro or municipal bus trip is R$8.20 (~$1.45). This price point reflects a steady climb from previous years as the city adjusts to rising energy costs. While cash is still accepted on most municipal buses, the system is rapidly moving toward a completely cashless environment. Metro stations and the VLT light rail already require digital payment methods. Therefore, travelers must prepare before reaching the turnstiles to avoid delays.\n\nMost turnstiles in the Metro and VLT systems now feature integrated NFC technology. Commuters can tap their international credit cards or smartphones directly at the gate for immediate access. This convenience eliminates the need for physical tickets for short-term visitors. However, residents and long-term expats find the RioCard Mais provides superior integration between different modes of transport. This card allows for discounted transfers between buses, trains, and ferries within specific time windows.\n\n### Budgeting for Monthly Transit Costs\n\nA daily Metro commuter making two trips per day will spend approximately R$360.80 (~$64) per month based on the 2026 projected rates. This figure is significantly lower than the cost of private car ownership in the city. When accounting for insurance, fuel, and the high cost of parking in districts like Leblon, car owners often face monthly expenses exceeding R$2,500 (~$440). Thus, public transport Rio de Janeiro remains the more viable economic choice for the majority of the city’s professional workforce. It offers a predictable monthly expense compared to the volatility of global oil prices.\n\n### Acquiring and Topping Up Your RioCard\n\nThe RioCard Mais is the primary tool for seamless transit across the city’s buses, trains, and ferries. Users can purchase these physical cards at major Metro stations, such as Carioca or Jardim Oceânico, and at dedicated RioCard kiosks. The initial card cost is typically R$4.00 (~$0.70). This small fee acts as a deposit for the physical hardware. For daily Ibovespa analysis, see [The Rio Times Market Reports](https://www.riotimesonline.com/premium-membership/).\n\nDigital management has become the standard for tech-savvy commuters in Brazil. The “RioCard Mais” app allows users to top up their balance using Pix or credit cards instantly. After payment, the credit must be validated at a terminal. These validation points are found on every bus and at station entrances. Instead of waiting in long queues at ticket windows, expats can manage their entire transit budget from a smartphone. This digital shift has reduced station congestion by 22% since its implementation began.\n\nLooking ahead, the city administration plans to expand the “Bilhete Único” integration to include private shuttle services by late 2026. This move aims to further reduce the financial burden on commuters traveling from the West Zone. Investors should monitor municipal legislative shifts regarding transit subsidies. These decisions will dictate the long-term stability of fare prices in the coming decade. Maintaining an efficient transport budget is a cornerstone of financial success for any expat living in the Marvelous City.\n\n## Safety, Reliability, and the Future of Rio’s Urban Mobility\n\nRio’s transit network in 2026 reflects a complex balance between aging infrastructure and aggressive security interventions. State officials recently expanded the “Segurança Presente” program to cover 15 major transit hubs, including the newly inaugurated Terminal Gentileza. This initiative deploys specialized police units to deter petty crime and assist commuters in high-traffic areas. Statistics from the Public Security Institute (ISP) show that passenger thefts on buses fell by 14% in the first half of 2026. However, safety remains a localized issue that varies significantly by neighborhood and transit type. Subway lines maintain a higher security rating than the SuperVia trains or certain North Zone bus corridors. Consequently, international travelers often prefer the Metro for daily commutes because of its controlled environments. Reliability on the **public transport Rio de Janeiro** provides has improved with the integration of centralized monitoring systems. These systems allow operators to respond faster to technical failures or security incidents.\n\n### Risk Management for the International Traveler\n\nExpats and investors should prioritize the Metro during off-peak hours for maximum safety and comfort. Lines 1 and 4 serve the South Zone and Barra da Tijuca; these are statistically the most secure routes in the city. Situational awareness is vital in crowded terminals like Carioca or Estácio, especially during the evening rush. Digital tools provide a necessary layer of predictability for the **public transport Rio de Janeiro** network. Apps like Moovit and Google Maps offer real-time tracking for the BRT and municipal bus fleets. These platforms help travelers avoid long waits at isolated stops where risks are higher. Commuters shouldn’t display high-value electronics during their trips. They should keep bags in front of their bodies in dense crowds to prevent pickpocketing. Additionally, the RioCard Mais system now allows for digital top-ups via WhatsApp. This feature reduces the time spent at physical kiosks where travelers might be vulnerable. For expats seeking a secure residential base near the Metro network, the Praia da Urca neighborhood offers a unique safety profile and stable property values that appeal to high-net-worth international residents. For daily updates on urban safety trends, [Go Premium](https://www.riotimesonline.com/premium-membership/) for deeper infrastructure analysis and risk reports.\n\n### The 2027 Outlook: What to Watch Next\n\nState legislators are currently debating the 2027-2030 Mobility Plan. This framework includes a proposed R$2.5 billion (~$450 million) investment in fleet electrification and station upgrades. A primary focus involves the long-delayed completion of the Gávea Metro station. Engineers estimate that finishing this link would add 20,000 daily passengers to Line 4, significantly easing traffic in the South Zone. Lawmakers are also discussing new transit subsidies to stabilize fares. Most municipal buses currently charge R$5.00 (~$0.90) per ride, but inflation pressures may force a revision by January 2027. Investors should monitor these legislative sessions closely. They will dictate the feasibility of future public-private partnerships for the VLT light rail. Specifically, the expansion of the VLT into the South Zone remains a significant topic for the 2028 fiscal cycle. Future plans also include the implementation of AI-driven traffic management to reduce bus congestion. Watch for the official budget release on December 15, 2026, to confirm these infrastructure priorities.\n\n## Strategic Mobility for Rio’s 2026 Economic Outlook\n\nRio de Janeiro’s infrastructure landscape is entering a transformative phase. The integration of the VLT and BRT systems creates a seamless corridor for the Porto Maravilha district. Consequently, property values in these connected zones are expected to rise. The MetrôRio continues to serve as the most reliable **public transport Rio de Janeiro** offers for professionals in the Centro and Leblon areas. Fares currently sit at R$7.50 (~$1.35) per trip. However, the municipal government plans to review these subsidies by December 2026. Therefore, investors must monitor these legislative shifts closely.\n\nLooking ahead, the market should focus on the 2027 tender for the completion of the Gávea metro station. This project will likely redefine transit times between the South Zone and the West Zone. Since 2009, The Rio Times has provided 17 years of trusted regional reporting. It’s the official source for the expat and diplomatic community in Brazil. To stay ahead of market shifts, [access exclusive Latin American business insights with a Premium Membership](https://www.riotimesonline.com/premium-membership/). The Rules, the Hidden Costs, and the Workarounds](https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil-import-export-rules/) · [Latin America Business News English: The 2026 Strategic Investment Report](https://www.riotimesonline.com/latin-america-business-news-english/)\n\n**More:** [Brazil news in English](https://www.riotimesonline.com/brazil/), every day from The Rio Times.\n\n## Frequently Asked Questions About Public Transport in Rio de Janeiro\n\n### Is public transport in Rio de Janeiro safe for foreigners in 2026?\n\nPublic transport Rio de Janeiro remains a viable and generally safe option for foreigners in 2026 if they maintain situational awareness. Data from the Rio de Janeiro Public Security Institute (ISP) indicates that petty theft is the most common issue on crowded lines. Consequently, travelers should avoid displaying expensive smartphones or jewelry during their journey. For daily Ibovespa analysis, see The Rio Times Market Reports.\n\n### How much does a Metro ticket cost in Rio de Janeiro right now?\n\nA single Metro ticket in Rio de Janeiro currently costs R$7.50 (~$1.35) as of late 2024. MetrôRio adjusts these fares annually based on regional inflation and operational expenditure requirements. Therefore, passengers should check the digital displays at station entrances for the most recent pricing updates. Most travelers prefer purchasing a rechargeable card to avoid long queues at ticket windows.\n\n### Can I pay for the bus with a credit card in Rio?\n\nMost municipal buses in Rio de Janeiro now accept contactless credit and debit cards for fare payment. This technological upgrade, managed by the municipal transport secretariat, covers over 90% of the city’s fleet. However, some older feeder lines in the North Zone still require a physical RioCard or cash. Consequently, carrying a small amount of Brazilian Real remains a prudent strategy for suburban travel.\n\n### What is the best way to get from Galeão Airport to Ipanema using public transport?\n\nThe most reliable public transport Rio de Janeiro route from Galeão Airport involves taking the BRT TransCarioca line to the Vicente de Carvalho station. From that point, passengers transfer directly to Metro Line 2 to reach the South Zone. Alternatively, the “Frescão” premium bus service offers a direct, air-conditioned link to Ipanema for approximately R$20.00 (~$3.60). For updates on Rio’s urban infrastructure projects, visit The Rio Times Infrastructure News.\n\n### Does the Rio de Janeiro Metro run 24 hours a day?\n\nThe Rio de Janeiro Metro does not operate 24 hours a day during standard business weeks. Trains typically run from 5:00 AM to midnight on Monday through Saturday, while Sunday service concludes at 11:00 PM. However, the city often extends these hours during major events like Carnival or New Year’s Eve. Consequently, late-night travelers must utilize ride-sharing applications or municipal night buses after the stations close.\n\n### What is the RioCard Mais and where can I buy one?\n\nThe RioCard Mais is a unified electronic payment card that works across all transit modes including ferries and trains. In addition, it allows for fare integration discounts when transferring between different systems within a specific timeframe. Commuters can purchase these cards at any Metro station or at dedicated vending machines located in major transport hubs. For deeper insights into Brazilian legislative shifts, read The Rio Times Political Briefing.\n\n### Are there “women only” cars on the Rio de Janeiro Metro?\n\nMetrôRio provides designated pink-labeled cars for women during peak commuting hours to enhance passenger safety and comfort. These carriages operate from 6:00 AM to 9:00 AM and from 5:00 PM to 8:00 PM on weekdays. Security personnel monitor these cars to ensure compliance with state law 4.733/06. However, these restrictions don’t apply on weekends or public holidays when all cars remain mixed.\n\n### How do I use the VLT tram in downtown Rio?\n\nPassengers use the VLT tram by tapping their RioCard Mais on the validation pillars located inside each carriage. The system operates on an honor basis because there are no physical turnstiles at the stops. However, ticket inspectors regularly board the trams to verify payments using handheld electronic readers. Failure to validate a card results in a fine of R$170.00 (~$30) according to municipal regulations.\n\nTravelers should watch for upcoming announcements regarding the expansion of the BRT TransBrasil corridor throughout 2026. This project aims to further integrate the city’s periphery with the central business district. Future shifts in interest rates may also influence municipal subsidies for transport fares in the coming fiscal year.\n\nIn depth\n\n- [Best places to live in Brazil (expat When you search for schedules and tickets, Omio will show you the best journey\n\n### Águia Branca\n\nAbout\n\nÁguia Branca is a prominent Brazilian bus company, established in 1946, offering extensive road transportation services across Brazil. It operates over 201 daily departures, connecting major cities like Rio de Janeiro, São Paulo, and Salvador, and serving more than 700 destinations across several states including Espírito Santo, Bahia, Minas Gerais, Rio de Janeiro, and São Paulo. The company is part of the larger Grupo Águia Branca, which also includes logistics and vehicle sales divisions. Águia Branca is known for its focus on comfort and safety, with various bus types such as Conventional, Executivo, Plus Service, and Double Leito, some offering amenities like Wi-Fi, USB outlets, and air conditioning.\n\nLuggage & cancellation policy\n\nAverage Águia Branca Duration\n\n3h 30m\n\nCheapest Águia Branca Price\n\n$13 (€11)\n\nÁguia Branca frequency\n\n21 a day\n\nLuggage included\n\nOne checked bag up to 30kg and one carry-on up to 5kg\n\nAdditional luggage\n\nAdditional luggage can be purchased with weight and size restrictions\n\nAmenities on board the bus\n\nWifi\n\nFood sold onboard\n\nSeat reservation\n\nToilets\n\nUSB plugs\n\nAccessibility\n\nWheelchair accessible buses available on select routes with advance notice required\n\nBikes\n\nBicycles accepted when properly packaged and disassembled\n\nPets\n\nSmall pets allowed in carriers with vaccination documents and health certificate\n\n[Learn more about Águia Branca](https://www.omio.com/companies/buses/aguia-branca-mjpu3)\n\n### Expresso Do Sul\n\nAbout\n\nExpresso do Sul is a Brazilian bus company based in Sao Paulo, offering intercity bus services primarily within Brazil. The company began operations in 2000 with a route between Florianopolis and Curitiba, expanding to include the popular Rio de Janeiro to Sao Paulo route in 2002. Expresso do Sul provides various bus classes, including Conventional, Executive, Semi-Sleeper, and Sleeper, with amenities such as air conditioning, toilets, and in some newer vehicles, Wi-Fi and USB charging ports. They are known for connecting major cities like Sao Paulo, Rio de Janeiro, Florianopolis, and Curitiba.\n\nLuggage & cancellation policy\n\nAverage Expresso Do Sul Duration\n\n6h 0m\n\nCheapest Expresso Do Sul Price\n\n$18 (€15)\n\nExpresso Do Sul frequency\n\n17 a day\n\nLuggage included\n\nOne checked bag up to 30kg and one carry-on up to 5kg\n\nAdditional luggage\n\nAdditional luggage can be purchased with weight and size restrictions\n\nAmenities on board the bus\n\nWifi\n\nFood sold onboard\n\nSeat reservation\n\nToilets\n\nUSB plugs\n\nAccessibility\n\nWheelchair accessible buses available on select routes with advance notice required\n\nBikes\n\nBicycles accepted when properly packaged and disassembled\n\nPets\n\nSmall pets allowed in carriers with vaccination documents and health certificate\n\n[Learn more about Expresso Do Sul](https://www.omio.com/companies/buses/expresso-do-sul-7vkqn)\n\n### Catarinense\n\nAbout\n\nCatarinense is a prominent Brazilian bus company, part of the JCA group, which collectively transports nearly 100 million passengers annually across South and Southeast Brazil. Based in Florianópolis, Catarinense offers over 274 daily departures to numerous cities including São Paulo, Rio de Janeiro, and Curitiba. The company provides various travel classes such as Conventional, Executive, Semi-sleeper, and Sleeper, all featuring reclining seats, air conditioning, and restrooms. Many newer buses also offer Wi-Fi and individual USB ports. Popular routes include São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, São Paulo to Foz do Iguaçu, and Florianópolis to São Paulo.\n\nLuggage & cancellation policy\n\nAverage Catarinense Duration\n\n5h 35m\n\nCheapest Catarinense Price\n\n$12 (€10)\n\nCatarinense frequency\n\n13 a day\n\nLuggage included\n\nOne checked bag up to 30kg and one carry-on up to 5kg\n\nAdditional luggage\n\nAdditional luggage allowed with extra fees\n\nAmenities on board the bus\n\nWifi\n\nFood sold onboard\n\nSeat reservation\n\nToilets\n\nUSB plugs\n\nAccessibility\n\nWheelchair accessibility available on select buses with advance notice required. São Geraldo de Viação. Initially managing regional routes in Minas Gerais and Bahia, the company expanded its services to include cities in Rio de Janeiro and Espírito Santo, and later São Paulo. With a fleet of over 350 buses, Viação Rio Doce transports approximately 12 million passengers annually. Travelers generally find it convenient to use cards, especially credit and debit cards, for most transactions as they are widely accepted in cities. However, carrying some cash is advisable for small purchases, street vendors, and in more remote areas where card acceptance may be limited.\n\n### What are the main safety concerns and scams to watch out for in Rio de Janeiro, RJ?\n\nIn Rio de Janeiro, travelers should be cautious of pickpocketing and petty theft, especially in crowded areas like Copacabana and Ipanema beaches, public transport, and during festivals. Avoid displaying valuables openly and be wary of unofficial taxi drivers. Common scams include overcharging by taxis, fake tour guides, and distraction techniques used to steal wallets or phones. Staying in well-lit, populated areas and using registered transportation options can help reduce risks.\n\n### What are some general packing tips for comfort and security?\n\nPack lightweight, versatile clothing suitable for the destination's climate, and use secure, lockable luggage to protect belongings. Keep essential items like travel documents, medication, and valuables in a carry-on bag for easy access. Use packing organizers or cubes to maximize space and maintain order. São Paulo is vast, and your ideal drop-off depends entirely on your onward plans:\n\n- **Terminal Rodoviário Tietê:** This is generally the best choice if you are transferring to Guarulhos Airport (GRU), heading to the northern or central districts, or catching a connecting bus to other states. Tietê connects directly to the Metro Blue Line (Linha 1-Azul).\n- **Terminal Rodoviário Barra Funda:** Ideal if your final destination is in São Paulo's western zone, or if you need quicker access to the Metro Red Line (Linha 3-Vermelha). It is also often a closer transfer point if you are heading to Congonhas Airport (CGH) via an app-based ride.\n\n## Be boarding-ready\n\nOnce your **bus Rio São Paulo** is booked, preparing for departure requires just a few quick checks. You can also explore more routes on our [main Brazil bus travel page](https://www.omio.com/buses/brazil-w3ne7).\n\n## Bus seat types in Brazil\n\nWhen booking a bus in Brazil, the seat class can make a big difference to your journey. Compare the available options to find the right balance of price, space, and recline—particularly for long-distance or overnight travel.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro Bus](https://assets.omio.design/lps/static-block/jpg/rio_de_janeiro_bus~static-block.jpg)\n\n## Seat type catalog\n\n| Class (English) | Portuguese Name | Comfort Expectation | Best For |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| Standard / Common | Convencional, Comum | Basic foamy seat, slight recline, usually air conditioning and a restroom depending on the operator | Shorter trips, lowest price |\n| Executive | Executivo | More comfortable than convencional, often with better recline and onboard comfort | Medium trips; budget comfort upgrade |\n| Semi-bed | Semi-leito, Semicama | Reclining seat, leg rest and footrest on many buses | Medium and long trips |\n| Semi-bed solo | Semi-leito Individual | Semi-leito comfort with no adjacent passenger | Medium and long trips; solo travellers |\n| Sleeper / Executive bed | Leito, Leito executivo | Wider seat, deeper recline, more space and often stronger onboard service | Long trips; overnight travel |\n| Sleeper solo | Leito Individual | Leito comfort with no adjacent passenger | Long overnight trips; solo travellers |\n| Full sleeper / Suite | Cama, Cama suite | Highest comfort tier, sometimes with up to 180° recline and more privacy | Longest trips; premium overnight travel |\n| Full sleeper solo | Cama Individual | Full-sleeper comfort with a solo-seat configuration | Premium solo travel |\n\n![Campo Comprido Bus](https://assets.omio.design/lps/static-block/jpg/campo_comprido_bus~static-block.jpg)\n\n### Standard\n\n**Convencional** or **Comum** is the basic, usually lowest-priced seat option. It is a practical choice for shorter trips or travellers prioritising price.\n\n### Executive and semi-sleeper\n\n**Executivo** offers extra comfort compared with Standard. **Semi-sleeper** seats usually provide more recline and leg support, making them a comfortable option for medium and longer journeys.\n\n![Brazil Executive Bus Seats](https://assets.omio.design/lps/static-block/jpg/brazil_bus_seats_one.jpg)\n\n### Sleeper\n\n**Leito** or **Leito Executivo** is a premium sleeper-style option, typically with a wider seat and deeper recline. It is well suited to long-distance and overnight travel.\n\n![Brazil Sleeper Bus Seats](https://assets.omio.design/lps/static-block/jpg/brazil_bus_seats_two.jpg)\n\n### Full sleeper\n\n**Cama** or **Cama Suite** is the highest comfort category. Where available, it may offer near-flat recline and additional privacy for overnight journeys.\n\n![Brazil Full Sleeper Bus Seats](https://assets.omio.design/lps/static-block/jpg/brazil_bus_seats_three.jpg)\n\n### Solo seats\n\nSome Semi-sleeper, Sleeper and Full sleeper services offer an **Individual** seat. These solo-seat options have no adjacent passenger.\n\nSeat types, features and availability vary by route and bus operator. Check the journey details before booking.\n\nWhich Rio boarding point should I choose?\n\nTerminal Novo Rio is the safest and most comprehensive choice, offering the most departure times and facilities. However, if your selected operator offers an alternative pick-up closer to your accommodation, you can select it using Omio's live filters—just ensure you verify the exact address before travel.\n\nShould I arrive at Tietê or Barra Funda?\n\nChoose Tietê if you need the Metro Blue Line, are heading to the north/center, or transferring to Guarulhos Airport. Choose Barra Funda if you need the Metro Red Line, are heading to the western districts, or require easier access to Congonhas Airport.\n\nWhere do I confirm my platform before boarding?\n\nPlatform assignments can usually be found on terminal departure screens upon arrival. Drive from São Paulo to Rio de Janeirocarcar267.4 mi\\\\\n\\\\\n4h 56m\\\\\n\\\\\n$44–63](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Drive)\n\n## São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro by bus\n\nCalendar1371Weekly Buses\n\nDuration6hAverage Duration\n\nTicket$18Cheapest Price\n\n[See schedules](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Bus)\n\n### Comparing Your Travel Options\n\nWhat is the cheapest way to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro?\n\nThe cheapest way to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro is to rideshare which costs $22 and takes 5h 10m.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Rideshare)\n\nWhat is the fastest way to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro?\n\nThe fastest way to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro is to fly which takes 2h 22m and costs $80 - $700.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Fly-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-De-Janeiro-Santos-Dumont)\n\nIs there a direct bus between São Paulo and Rio de Janeiro?\n\nYes, there is a direct bus departing from Tietê Bus Terminal and arriving at Terminal Rodoviário Novo Rio. Services depart every four hours, and operate every day. The journey takes approximately 6h.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Fly-Sao-Paulo-Congonhas-to-Rio-De-Janeiro-Santos-Dumont)\n\nHow far is it from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro?\n\nThe distance between São Paulo and Rio de Janeiro is 235 miles. The road distance is 269 miles.\n\n[Get driving directions](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Drive)\n\nHow do I travel from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro without a car?\n\nThe best way to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro without a car is to bus via Jacareí which takes 5h 4m and costs $19 - $27.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Bus-via-Jacare%C3%AD)\n\nHow long does it take to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro?\n\nIt takes approximately 2h 40m to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, including transfers.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Fly-Sao-Paulo-Congonhas-to-Rio-De-Janeiro-Santos-Dumont)\n\nWhere do I catch the São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro bus from?\n\nSão Paulo to Rio de Janeiro bus services, operated by Passaro Marron, depart from Tietê Bus Terminal station.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Bus-via-Jacare%C3%AD)\n\nBus or fly from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro?\n\nThe best way to get from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro is to fly which takes 2h 40m and costs $80 - $650. Alternatively, you can bus via Jacareí, which costs $19 - $27 and takes 5h 4m.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro)\n\nWhere does the São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro bus arrive?\n\nSão Paulo to Rio de Janeiro bus services, operated by Passaro Marron, arrive at Jacareí station.\n\n[More details](https://www.rome2rio.com/map/S%C3%A3o-Paulo/Rio-de-Janeiro?route=Bus-via-Jacare%C3%AD)\n\nCan I drive from São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro?\n\nYes, the driving distance between São Paulo to Rio de Janeiro is 269 miles.       {
        "url": "https://blog.urbanadventures.com/complete-guide-rio-de-janeiro-travel/",
        "title": "The complete guide to Rio de Janeiro travel - Urban Adventures",
        "description": "The cost will range from USD 2 to 5. Expect to pay anywhere from USD 50 to 100, You can buy tickets from several different operators, and ...",
        "position": 5,
        "markdown": "![Rio de Janeiro Christ the Redeemer](https://blog.urbanadventures.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/02/rio_christ-redeemer.jpg)\n\nFeb\n\n11\n\n# The complete guide to: Rio de Janeiro travel\n\nFebruary 11, 2015\n\n![](https://secure.gravatar.com/avatar/36a2cb209fe8e4d4f95560bdff461adb7ae78a474a696f457e28b4e5597061b5?s=100&d=mm&r=g)\n\nUrban Adventures\n\n[Brazil](https://blog.urbanadventures.com/category/destinations/latin-america/brazil/)\n\n[Complete Guides](https://blog.urbanadventures.com/category/features/complete-guides/)\n\n[Latin America](https://blog.urbanadventures.com/category/destinations/latin-america/)\n\n[Rio de Janeiro](https://blog.urbanadventures.com/category/destinations/latin-america/brazil/rio-de-janeiro/)\n\nSo you’re travelling to [Rio de Janeiro](http://www.urbanadventures.com/destination/rio-de-janeiro-tours \"Urban Adventures Rio de Janeiro tours\") — smart choice! This guide will give you everything you need to know about visiting Rio, from getting to and from the airport, to the best Brazilian movies you should see, to the cool local spots you should check out.\n\n### **Getting to and from GIG**\n\nPublic transit is an option for getting into the city, especially if you’re headed to Barra da Tijuca (the neighbourhood where the Olympics will take place in 2016). Hop on the Transcarioca Bus right from the international terminal; at Barra’s last stop, grab a cab to reach your final destination. If you are heading to areas such as Ipanema and Copacabana, the public option is the Real Bus, which takes you from the international airport, and drives through both the main bus station and domestic airport before arriving at the beaches area on the southern side. Be aware it only goes along the coast — therefore if you are staying in a hotel that’s not oceanfront, you’ll have to drag your luggage from the closest stop. The cost will range from USD 2 to 5.\n\nAlternately, you can take a taxi into the city. There are two types of taxis: regular yellow cabs, which will cost you about USD 30 to the southern neighbourhoods, and USD 45 to the western side of Rio. There are also radio taxis (they can be green, red, blue, white, or silver in colour), which charge a fixed price depending on the drop-off location. Expect to pay anywhere from USD 50 to 100, depending on where you’re being dropped off. Both types of taxis are available from inside the terminal, and even before you leave the restricted area right after you pass through customs. They’re safe, but avoid accepting taxi offers from independent drivers, even if they’re inside the terminal.\n\nIf you’re the type who likes to make arrangements before you arrive, [Green Path Transfers](http://www.greenpathtransfers.com/ \"Green Path Transfers Rio de Janeiro\") is a good option for private transportation from the airport.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro runner](http://www.urbanadventures.com/blog/wp-content/uploads/rio_fitness.jpg)\n\n### **Getting around Rio**\n\n**Metro:** The [MetrôRio](http://www.metrorio.com.br/ \"Metro Rio de Janeiro\") is by far your best option for getting around the city (of course, that’s assuming you’re staying close to a station — but in general, the southern hotels are all close to a subway stop). The fare is a flat rate of BRL 3.50 (USD 1.50) per trip, regardless of how far you’re travelling, and you buy your ticket from the ticket offices in the metro stations. The service runs from 5am to midnight on weekdays and Saturdays, and 7am to 11pm on Sundays and holidays.\n\n**Taxi:** Taxis are easily available, and offer metered rates (expect to pay about BRL 40 (USD 14.50) for a ride from the southern beaches to the downtown area). They’re generally safe, but be aware of common scams. Your best option is to book via the [Taxibeat](http://taxibeat.com.br/ \"Taxibeat Rio de Janeiro\") or [Easy Taxi](http://www.easytaxi.com/br \"Easy Taxi Rio de Janeiro\") apps.\n\n**Walking:** Of course, the best way to see the city is on foot! The southern beaches are safe for walking around, so get out and explore! And while the Lapa area is famed for its raucous nightlife, it’s best to avoid walking the backstreets in that district.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro beach](http://www.urbanadventures.com/blog/wp-content/uploads/rio_beach.jpg)\n\n### **Things to do in Rio**\n\nYou’re in Brazil, right? So of course you absolutely must watch a football game at [Maracanã Stadium](http://www.maracana.com/ \"Maracana Rio de Janeiro\"). There is nothing locals love more than a good football match, so join the masses and get swept up in the crazy, wild, oh-so-fun atmosphere. Ticket prices vary with the season, but expect to pay about USD 50 per ticket. You can buy tickets year-round at the venue, from the ticketing office.\n\nIf you’re more about music than sports, a visit to the Samba rehearsals at [Salgueiro](http://www.salgueiro.com.br/ \"Salgueiro Rio de Janeiro\") is where you want to be. Besides Carnival itself, the Samba rehearsals are Rio’s most honoured cultural experience. You can buy tickets at the venue’s ticket office (prices vary with the season), and your best time to check out the spectacle is from July to February, in the lead-up to Carnival.\n\nAnd if you’re coming for Carnival itself (and if you are, lucky you!), you’ll want to watch the [Carnival Parade](http://liesa.globo.com/ \"Rio de Janeiro Carnival Parade\") at the Sambadrome. Some say it’s the biggest party on Earth, and we tend to agree. It’s a 10-hour show and at true one-in-a-lifetime experience. Six Samba schools parade for 80 minutes each night, with each school bringing over 3,000 participants, all singing non-stop along with gorgeous floats and costumes, led by more than 200 drummers and a full band. You can buy tickets from several different operators, and prices can range from USD 100 to 1,500, depending on your seat and whether you opt for a grandstand seat or all-inclusive box seat. It can be pricey, but it’s also pretty much guaranteed to be the most exciting experience ever!\n\n### **Rio de Janeiro Day Tours**\n\nLooking for more things to do? Urban Adventures offers several [day tours in Rio de Janeiro](http://www.urbanadventures.com/destination/rio-de-janeiro-tours), all led by local experts that will give you a taste for local life in the city:\n\n**[Santa Teresa Discovery](http://www.urbanadventures.com/Santa-Teresa-Discovery)**\n\nWhen you’ve had enough of the sizzling bods and football heroics of the beach scene, join this Rio de Janiero tour which will take you on a retreat up to the classic locale of Santa Teresa for some arts, culture, history, and architecture.\n\n**[Corcovado and Christ Statue Tour](http://www.urbanadventures.com/rio-de-janeiro-tour-corcovado-christ-statue-and-favela-tour)**\n\nVisit Brazil’s most heavy hitting icons in one day on this Rio de Janiero tour. This could just be the ultimate, big city travel hack!\n\n**[Total Rio Tour](http://www.urbanadventures.com/rio-de-janeiro-tour-rio-total-tour)**\n\nDon’t know what to do with a full day in Rio? How does a visit to Christ the Redeemer and a ride on Rio’s last remaining tram sound? Join this Rio de Janeiro tour to see the world-famous diversity of Brazil’s second largest city.\n\n### **Rio de Janeiro on the big screen**\n\n[_Tropa de Elite_](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ELlkWtNWyKY \"YouTube: Elite Squad trailer\") ( _Elite Squad_) and its sequel [_Tropa de Elite: O inimigo agora é outro_](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_gnJB10WTpE \"YouTube: Elite Squad: Enemy Within trailer\") ( _Elite Squad: The Enemy Within_) are two fascinating, astonishing, and intense films that examine Rio de Janeiro’s notorious _favelas_. The latter broke Brazilian movie records: the fifth biggest opening in Brazilian history, and the biggest opening for a Brazilian film, as well as the highest-grossing film and largest box office ticket seller in Brazil. No worries if your Portuguese is rusty — it’s possible to find both films with English subtitles.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro favela](https://blog.urbanadventures.com/wp-content/uploads/2015/02/rio_favela.jpg)\n\n### **Rio de Janeiro on record**\n\nBlast some Bossa Nova! [_Garota de Ipanema_](http://musica.com.br/artistas/vinicius-de-moraes/m/garota-de-ipanema/letra.html \"Musica.com: Garota de Ipanema\") is the best-known Bossa Nova song in the world, and was even recorded into an English version by none other than legend Frank Sinatra.\n\n### **Rio de Janeiro in books**\n\nBrush up on your Samba (and your Portuguese) with _Desde que o Samba é Samba_. This book explains the origin of the famed Carioca musical style, and puts it into a historical and social context.\n\nOr, for some non-guide-like tips, check out the very non-guide-like guide [_Rio for Partiers_](http://www.rioforpartiers.com/ \"Rio for Partiers\"). It’s got the scoop on exclusive local parties, and also gives you a few tips on how to avoid looking like a tourist.\n\n![Rio de Janeiro Carnival parade](http://www.urbanadventures.com/blog/wp-content/uploads/rio_carnival_street-1024x1024.jpg)\n\n### **Get in touch with us**\n\nIf you have any questions about your trip, give us a call at +55 21 99154-4785, or email us at [info@riodejaneirourbanadventures.com](mailto:info@riodejaneirourbanadventures.com \"Email Rio de Janeiro Urban Adventures\").\n\n### **Get social with us**\n\nWanna be friends? You can find us on [Facebook](https://www.facebook.com/riodejaneirourbanadventures \"Facebook: Rio de Janeiro Urban Adventures\") and [Instagram](http://instagram.com/riourbanadventures \"Rio de Janeiro Urban Adventures Instagram\"). We’re always around town posting about our adventures, so if you want to know what’s happening in real time, we’re your guys!\n\n**_And then, if you want to book more Urban Adventures tours in Rio, [go right ahead](http://www.urbanadventures.com/destination/rio-de-janeiro-tours)!_**\n\n## RELATED POSTS\n\nWhat lockdown has been like in Havana\n\nA Simple Guide to Thai Temple Etiquette\n\nLonely Planet Best in Travel 2020: What not to miss according to locals\n\nThe 10 Most Romantic Tours in North America\n\nBest Street Food in Havana: Coco Glasé in Plaza Vieja",

      {
        "url": "https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/travel-by-bus-sao-paulo-to-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/",
        "title": "How To Travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil by Bus",
        "description": "[Skip to content](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/travel-by-bus-sao-paulo-to-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/#content)\n\n# How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil [and vice-versa]\nBy [Two Monkeys Travel - Contributor](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/author/twomonkeystravelforum/)\n\n|  March 3, 2025\n\n[Brazil](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/brazil-best-luxury-hotel/) is the largest country in South America and Latin America. It has many gorgeous cities that each has its own charms and uniqueness. Two of the popular cities people visit are Sao Paulo and Rio de Janeiro. Here’s a guide on how to **travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro**, Brazil, by bus, and vice versa.\n\nTable of Contents\n\n[Toggle](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/travel-by-bus-sao-paulo-to-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/#)\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 07](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-07.jpeg)Photo by [Jonathan Borba](https://unsplash.com/@jonathanborba?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText) on [Unsplash](https://unsplash.com/s/photos/brazil-bus?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText) >\n\n[Sao Paulo](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/awesome-things-to-do-in-sao-paulo/) is the most populous city in the Americas and the wealthiest in Brazil. Aside from gorgeous buildings and parks, you can [enjoy delicious food](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/best-brazillian-food-dishes/) and festive events. [Rio de Janeiro](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/awesome-things-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/) is the second-most populous city in Brazil. With gorgeous beaches and many cultural spots, it’s the most visited in South America. If you are in Brazil, you should stop in these two cities if you are here for a week!\n\n[box] **Other articles you can read:**\n\n[/box]\n\n## Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro by Direct Bus\nMany bus companies are catering to travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro. In Sao Paulo, the bus is at Tiete or Barra Funda Bus Terminal. Here are a few companies operating direct buses:\n\n**[AguiaBranca](https://www.aguiabranca.com.br/)**\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 02](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-02.png)\n\n- The bus terminal is at Tiete\n- Travel Time is 6 hours and 30 minutes\n- Convecional Bus Tickets costs BRL 69 and for Leito Cama it can cost about BRL 189\n- Free Cancellation (please read the terms)\n- Tickets can be purchased online\n- You may need an ID to board\n- There are routes as early as 6:00 AM to late 2:00 AM, in every hour or two hours\n\n- The bus terminal in Sao Paulo is at Barra Funda to Rio de Janeiro – Rodoviária Novo Rio\n- Travel Time is 6 hours and 20 minutes\n- Convecional Bus Tickets costs BRL 69.99, and for Leito it can cost about BRL 159.99\n- Tickets can be purchased online\n\n- The route is from Sao Paulo – Tiete Bus Terminal to Rodoviária Novo Rio\n- Schedules are on the picture\n- Convencional Bus Seats are about BRL 98.31 while it is BRL 180.18 for Sleeper buses\n\n## Bus Terminals\n![How To Travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil by Bus](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-09.jpeg)Photo by [lubasi](https://www.flickr.com/photos/lubasi/) CC BY-SA 2.0\n\n**Sao Paulo – Tiete Rio (Rodoviária Tiete)**\n\nCruzeiro do Sul, 1800 – Santana\n\n+ 55 (11) 3866-1100\n\nTiete Bus Station is the biggest bus terminal in Latin America. Not only do they have buses that can travel to cities in Brazil but also to other Latin American countries. There are also shower cubicles in the terminal. A subway station is also connected here.\n\n**São Paulo (Barra Funda) Bus Station**\n\nAuro Soares de Moura Andrade, 664 – Barra Funda\n\n+55 (11) 3866-1100\n\nThe second busiest bus station in Sao Paul is Estacao Barra Funda. More than 30 bus companies are operating here going to more than 500 different places in the country. It is also connected to Palmeiras Subway Station.\n\n**Rio de Janeiro Bus Terminal – Rodoviária Novo Rio**\n\nAddress: Francisco Bicalho, 1 – Santo Cristo\n\n+55 (21) 3213-1800\n\nThe main Bus terminal in Rio is Rodovaria Novo Rio. There are a thousand routes with 145 interstate lines and 6 international bus lines. You will need to ride another bus to go to the nearest subway station.\n\n![7 Awesome Things to do in Rio de Janeiro Brazil](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/07/7-Awesome-Things-to-do-in-Rio-de-Janeiro-scaled.jpg)Photo by [Raphael Nogueira](https://unsplash.com/@phaelnogueira?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText) on [Unsplash](https://unsplash.com/s/photos/brazil?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText)\n\nThat’s how you can _travel by bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro_. An hour flight from these cities would cost the same or maybe more expensive than a Lieto Seat. If you want to save time and money, you can also try to have a trip during the night at a semi-bed or executive – sleep comfortably on the bus and wake in a new city!\n\n[line]\n\n**About the Writer**\n\n![Lyza Paloma](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/02/Lyza-Paloma.jpg)\n\nHey, I’m Lyza! I once was a person who just imagined going to places “one day” but decided to pursue my dreams. My first travel abroad was in Japan, solo, last 2018, and fell in love with the experience. I aim to visit 10 countries before turning 30 and 2 new places in the Philippines every year. Besides traveling, I love organizing trips, taking pictures, reading, and making new friends. Follow my adventures through my [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/lyzappyza/).\n\n[line]\n\n**Are you on Pinterest?** **Pin these!**\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil [and vice versa]](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/06/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-and-vice-versa1-380x570.png)![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil [and vice versa]](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/06/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-and-vice-versa2-380x570.png)\n\nWe use cookies to ensure that we give you the best experience on our website. If you continue to use this site we will assume that you are happy with it.OkNoPrivacy policy\n\nA2A",
        "position": 6,
        "markdown": "[Skip to content](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/travel-by-bus-sao-paulo-to-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/#content)\n\n# How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil \\[and vice-versa\\]\n\nBy [Two Monkeys Travel - Contributor](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/author/twomonkeystravelforum/ \"View all posts by Two Monkeys Travel - Contributor\")\n\n\\|  March 3, 2025\n\n[Brazil](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/brazil-best-luxury-hotel/) is the largest country in South America and Latin America. It has many gorgeous cities that each has its own charms and uniqueness. Two of the popular cities people visit are Sao Paulo and Rio de Janeiro. Here’s a guide on how to **travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro**, Brazil, by bus, and vice versa.\n\nTable of Contents\n\n[Toggle](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/travel-by-bus-sao-paulo-to-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/#)\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 07](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-07.jpeg)Photo by [Jonathan Borba](https://unsplash.com/@jonathanborba?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText) on [Unsplash](https://unsplash.com/s/photos/brazil-bus?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText) >\n\n[Sao Paulo](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/awesome-things-to-do-in-sao-paulo/) is the most populous city in the Americas and the wealthiest in Brazil. Aside from gorgeous buildings and parks, you can [enjoy delicious food](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/best-brazillian-food-dishes/) and festive events. [Rio de Janeiro](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/awesome-things-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/) is the second-most populous city in Brazil. With gorgeous beaches and many cultural spots, it’s the most visited in South America. If you are in Brazil, you should stop in these two cities if you are here for a week!\n\n\\[box\\] **Other articles you can read:**\n\n- [How To Travel Around The Coast of Brazil; Guide from Rio de Janeiro to Paraty](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/cruising-the-coast-of-brazil/)\n- [7 Awesome Things to do in Rio de Janeiro, Brazil](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/awesome-things-rio-de-janeiro-brazil/)\n- [Brazilian Food: 12 Authentic Brazilian Dishes That You Need to Try](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/best-brazillian-food-dishes/)\n- [DIY Travel Guide to Brazil: Travel Solo for 16 Days](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/diy-travel-guide-to-brazil-travel-solo-for-16-days/)\n- [7 Awesome Things to do in Sao Paulo](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/awesome-things-to-do-in-sao-paulo/)\n\n\\[/box\\]\n\n## Types of Bus in Brazil\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 06](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-06.jpeg)Photo by sergio souza on [Unsplash](https://unsplash.com/s/photos/sao-paulo?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText)\n\nThere are many types of buses in Brazil, and you can choose depending on which you feel is more comfortable. Selections are usually in Portuguese, so I hope this could help you.\n\n- **Convencional** – it’s a bus with an ordinary seat, some may have a reclining feature. It’s the cheapest of all the bus types and good for short-distance trips.\n- **Executivo** – Executive Type Bus. It’s air-conditioned with comfortable reclined seats. There may be bathrooms, and some companies may offer free water.\n- **Semi-Leito** – Semi-Bed bus and can be reclined almost fully It’s air-conditioned and with bathrooms. There may be extra services like free blankets, pillows, and a fridge for your trips.\n- **Leito Cama** – First Class or Sleeper Bus is the most expensive but most comfortable out of all buses. It’s ideal for long trips. Seats are more spacious and can be fully reclined, and you will feel almost at home.\n\nPlease also note that there might be some differences in classes for each bus type. Like there are only 4 types for AguiaBranca but 5 types for Catarinense (with Leito – chairs reclines up to 150 degrees then Cama with chairs reclining up to 180 degrees)\n\n## Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro by Direct Bus\n\nMany bus companies are catering to travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro. In Sao Paulo, the bus is at Tiete or Barra Funda Bus Terminal. Here are a few companies operating direct buses:\n\n**[AguiaBranca](https://www.aguiabranca.com.br/)**\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 02](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-02.png)\n\n- The bus terminal is at Tiete\n- Travel Time is 6 hours and 30 minutes\n- Convecional Bus Tickets costs BRL 69 and for Leito Cama it can cost about BRL 189\n- Free Cancellation (please read the terms)\n- Tickets can be purchased online\n- You may need an ID to board\n- There are routes as early as 6:00 AM to late 2:00 AM, in every hour or two hours\n\n**[Catarinense](http://www.catarinense.com.br/)**\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 03](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-03.png)\n\n- The bus terminal in Sao Paulo is at Barra Funda to Rio de Janeiro – Rodoviária Novo Rio\n- Travel Time is 6 hours and 20 minutes\n- Convecional Bus Tickets costs BRL 69.99, and for Leito it can cost about BRL 159.99\n- Tickets can be purchased online\n\n**[1001](https://www.autoviacao1001.com.br/)**\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 01](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-01.png)\n\n- The route is from Sao Paulo – Tiete Bus Terminal to Rodoviária Novo Rio\n- Schedules are on the picture\n- Convencional Bus Seats are about BRL 98.31 while it is BRL 180.18 for Sleeper buses\n\n## Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro with a Stop\n\nIf you think 6 hours is too long, even though with a comfortable seat, then you can stop at a town. Though [Paraty](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/cruising-the-coast-of-brazil/) is a gorgeous place, it’s quite a long journey there. So if you want to break down 6 hours into half, go to Lorena.\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil 08](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-08.jpeg)Photo by [Fred Schinke](https://www.flickr.com/photos/fredfoto/) CC BY-ND 2.0\n\nLorena is a town in Sau Paulo. One great place to see there is the Serra da Bocaina National Park. You can ride a bus via [Cometa](http://www.viacaocometa.com.br/) from Sao Paulo – Tiete to Lorena. Travel time is 3 hours via Convencional Seats costing BRL 57.14.\n\nFrom Lorena, you can catch a bus to Rio de Janeiro. It will take 4 hours and will cost about BRL 56 for an executive seat with [Sampaio](http://www.viacaosampaio.com.br/).\n\n## Bus Terminals\n\n![How To Travel from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil by Bus](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-09.jpeg)Photo by [lubasi](https://www.flickr.com/photos/lubasi/) CC BY-SA 2.0\n\n**Sao Paulo – Tiete Rio (Rodoviária Tiete)**\n\nCruzeiro do Sul, 1800 – Santana\n\n\\+ 55 (11) 3866-1100\n\nTiete Bus Station is the biggest bus terminal in Latin America. Not only do they have buses that can travel to cities in Brazil but also to other Latin American countries. There are also shower cubicles in the terminal. A subway station is also connected here.\n\n**São Paulo (Barra Funda) Bus Station**\n\nAuro Soares de Moura Andrade, 664 – Barra Funda\n\n+55 (11) 3866-1100\n\nThe second busiest bus station in Sao Paul is Estacao Barra Funda. More than 30 bus companies are operating here going to more than 500 different places in the country. It is also connected to Palmeiras Subway Station.\n\n**Rio de Janeiro Bus Terminal – Rodoviária Novo Rio**\n\nAddress: Francisco Bicalho, 1 – Santo Cristo\n\n+55 (21) 3213-1800\n\nThe main Bus terminal in Rio is Rodovaria Novo Rio. There are a thousand routes with 145 interstate lines and 6 international bus lines. You will need to ride another bus to go to the nearest subway station.\n\n![7 Awesome Things to do in Rio de Janeiro Brazil](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/07/7-Awesome-Things-to-do-in-Rio-de-Janeiro-scaled.jpg)Photo by [Raphael Nogueira](https://unsplash.com/@phaelnogueira?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText) on [Unsplash](https://unsplash.com/s/photos/brazil?utm_source=unsplash&utm_medium=referral&utm_content=creditCopyText)\n\nThat’s how you can _travel by bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro_. An hour flight from these cities would cost the same or maybe more expensive than a Lieto Seat. If you want to save time and money, you can also try to have a trip during the night at a semi-bed or executive – sleep comfortably on the bus and wake in a new city!\n\n\\[line\\]\n\n**About the Writer**\n\n![Lyza Paloma](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2020/02/Lyza-Paloma.jpg)\n\nHey, I’m Lyza! I once was a person who just imagined going to places “one day” but decided to pursue my dreams. My first travel abroad was in Japan, solo, last 2018, and fell in love with the experience. I aim to visit 10 countries before turning 30 and 2 new places in the Philippines every year. Besides traveling, I love organizing trips, taking pictures, reading, and making new friends. Follow my adventures through my [Instagram](https://www.instagram.com/lyzappyza/).\n\n\\[line\\]\n\n**Are you on Pinterest? Pin these!**\n\n![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil [and vice versa]](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/06/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-and-vice-versa1-380x570.png)![How To Travel by Bus from Sao Paulo to Rio de Janeiro, Brazil [and vice versa]](https://twomonkeystravelgroup.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/06/How-To-Travel-by-Bus-from-Sao-Paulo-to-Rio-de-Janeiro-Brazil-and-vice-versa2-380x570.png)\n\nWe use cookies to ensure that we give you the best experience on our website. If you continue to use this site we will assume that you are happy with it.OkNoPrivacy policy\n\nA2A",
